# Homelab TODO Backlog

> Restructured **2026-08-17** (and trimmed 2026-08: struck rows removed; deploy-gated live-verify moved to [`deployment-tasks.md`](deployment-tasks.md) per-phase blocks). Human decisions moved to the front, work organized into domain modules, deferred items parked. Done items live in the owning `docs/*.md` + git history (frozen `reports/changelog.md` is archive-only); conventions → [`CONVENTIONS.md`](CONVENTIONS.md). Single source for planned work + open decisions (HD-XX).

---

## 0. How this backlog works

- **One row = one outcome.** New work gets `HD-<next>` and links its owning `docs/*.md`.
- **Mint space.** Below the registry top, an `HD-` number is a backlog id; the `9000–9999` block is **tool-fixture namespace** and no row is ever minted into it. The reason is structural, not tidiness: the next-id scan is deliberately tree-wide (a narrow scan once handed out an id a live row already held), and a tree-wide scan reads a classifier's own synthetic tokens as evidence — so the fixtures mint inside the block, and [`scripts/next-hd.sh`](scripts/next-hd.sh) filters it. Re-derive the next id by **running** that script, never by grepping for `HD-`, and let [`scripts/gate/check_hd_mint_space.py`](scripts/gate/check_hd_mint_space.py) keep both halves true (a fixture id outside the block reddens, a row inside it reddens).
- **A row is not a changelog.** It says **what is still missing** and, usefully, **how to do it** — the command, the gotcha, the measurement you would otherwise re-take. Everything *durable* (a finding, a root cause, how a subsystem behaves, what was shipped and verified) goes into the owning `docs/*.md` and the commit, and the row keeps only a link to it. So a healthy edit that lands a discovery makes the row **shorter**, not longer, and a row whose `⏳` list is empty is deleted, not decorated with a ✅. Litmus test before saving: does this sentence help whoever finishes the task, or does it only describe what already happened? The first goes in the row, the second in the doc, and neither twice.
- **Priority (P):** 1 = highest (how hot), module = what domain it touches. Priority is per-row, modules group.
- **Executors:** `AI` · `AI + gate` (human checkpoint) · `AI + Human` (joint) · `Human` (blocks).
- **Lifecycle:** open → (decided) → done — ONE pattern (CONVENTIONS §4): a **fully-done** row is DELETED from this file (record lives in the owning doc + git history); an **IaC-done-but-deploy-gated** row STAYS with a `⏳` tail (trimmed to pending items) until the live verify closes it. Deferred → park section. **Decisions:** a decided item is written **once** to its owning `docs/*.md` (+ the `<domain>-rejected.md` log where applicable) and the full rationale is **removed** from `todo.md` — §1 shows only still-open decisions/purchases (compact pointer, no struck duplicate). Historical decisions + done rows live in the frozen `reports/changelog.md` (archive-only).
- **Conventions / onboarding:** consolidated rule index + 10-step service-onboarding checklist in [`CONVENTIONS.md`](CONVENTIONS.md).
- **Service stages:** service-onboarding rows (per-checklist tasks) carry **`Stage: N/10`** in the bold title = current Service-onboarding checklist step ([CONVENTIONS.md](CONVENTIONS.md) §5). `10/10` = deployed + verified; only then does a row close. Non-service tasks (network/fix/decision) carry no stage marker.

| HD-1097 | 2 | AI + owner | 1 | **`\\nas\media` mounts over the LAN cable and not over Wi-Fi — the LAN zone had no host record, so the UNC rode the NetBIOS/LLMNR broadcast fallback** — measured 2026-10-07 on the owner's laptop (Wi-Fi on VLAN 10 with a dynamic lease — its Wi-Fi MAC is randomized, so the `laptop-domen` reservation never binds there — Samba healthy: `smbd`+`nmbd` active, ARP entry for the wireless client, 445 open both ways): `nas.kogler.si` → **NXDOMAIN with `aa`** on both home resolvers while `media`/`cockpit-nas` answered normally, and DHCP delivered **no option 15** either (no `domain=` anywhere in `rb4011_converge.rsc.j2`, `/ip dhcp-server option` empty) although network-vlans.md §DHCP said it was set on every network. Root cause + the reusable diagnostic (**IP mounts, name does not ⇒ resolution, not Samba**) live in [network-dns.md](docs/network-dns.md) §Local Name Resolution & mDNS and [storage.md](docs/storage.md) §Samba; `switch`/`ilo` stay unpublished by rule (Mgmt-only address ⇒ a black hole for a Home client). ✅ **Applied 2026-10-07:** seeded with `bash scripts/ansible/ansible-run.sh playbooks/dns-seed.yml` (`nas.kogler.si` now answers the `nas` Home address from the SSOT on the oldsrv and Pi instances, service names unchanged) and option 15 attached on the RB4011 (`dhcp-domain`, `code=15`, `raw-value=6b6f676c65722e7369`, on dhcp-mgmt/10/20/30/40/50). ⚠ **The mechanism is not `domain=`** — RouterOS 7 has no such property (`set … domain=` aborts the import with `Script Error: bad parameter domain`): option 15 rides an `/ip dhcp-server option` row referenced by each server's `dhcp-option`, and the hex value must be written INLINE (`:local dom "0x…"` + `value=$dom` reports success and writes an EMPTY value — the first import shipped exactly that). Read back with `print detail`/`raw-value` and the `:foreach … get` form — a filtered print reports *unset* when the value **is** set (HD-1095). The delta is folded into `rb4011_converge.rsc.j2` and its file deleted; `roles/router` owns nothing here on purpose. ⏳ **Acceptance is the laptop, not a `dig`:** `ipconfig /renew` → `ipconfig /all` carries `Connection-specific DNS Suffix : kogler.si`, `Resolve-DnsName nas.kogler.si` → the `nas` SSOT Home address, and `net use W: \\nas\media /user:domen` mounts **on Wi-Fi**; one family machine on each link proves it is not laptop-specific. ⚠ A parity-fixture note for the next person who adds a name here: `check_zone_kogler_si_parity.py` goes RED on ANY added name and `capture_zone_kogler_si_golden.py` refuses to re-capture a derived zone — the fixture moves by hand, in the same commit, with the reason in the message. |

Row stays open on owner timing. |
| HD-418 | 2 | AI + gate | 1 | **HA rejects anything oldsrv proxies — and the trust list HA enforces is not the one the YAML renders** — a request carrying `X-Forwarded-For` from a peer outside the effective list gets `400 Bad Request` (proven 2026-09-20: `Host: ha.kogler.si` + XFF → 400, without → 200), so the standby edge's `ha` router has **never worked** and would fail the same way in a takeover; the tailnet router only sidesteps it by stripping the forwarding headers. ✅ **DECIDED 2026-09-21 (owner): add `oldsrv_home_ip` to `ha_trusted_proxies`, in a planned window — it restarts the smart-home controller**; ✅ **Authorized 2026-10-08 (owner): the HA restart may run unattended in the overnight window** (short voice outage; read HA's active conversations first). ⚠ **Premise corrected 2026-10-09:** `ha_trusted_proxies` has listed `oldsrv_home_ip`/32 (`group_vars/all/main.yml`, SSOT `network-addresses-generated.md`) since `c9c36f23` (2026-09-11) and it is what renders the list (`configuration.yaml.j2:12`), so "the var omits oldsrv" stopped being the repo's state nine days BEFORE the 400 was proven. ⚠ **Premise corrected again 2026-10-19, and this one invalidates the method the row prescribed:** on this instance the rendered `configuration.yaml` is **not** what HA enforces. `yaml_migration_done: true`, and `.storage/http` carries the live list — both `stable` and `pending` hold two entries (the oldsrv `traefik-public` bridge default and the Pi's own host-IP edge, per `group_vars/all/main.yml`) with `pending.error = "not_promoted"` and the object's mtime at the 2026-09-03 11:10 migration — while the rendered YAML lists a third, the oldsrv Home-IP. So `grep -A6 trusted_proxies configuration.yaml`, the read this row told the next session to take, can neither prove nor fix the symptom, and neither can a converge of the YAML. ✅ **Corrected at both ends 2026-10-19:** [smart-home-failover.md](docs/smart-home-failover.md) §Caveats now names the storage object as the source and the **request** as the instrument (`200` = trusted, `400 untrusted proxy X` = prints the exact address that is missing), [services-traefik.md](docs/services-traefik.md) §Certification carries the same warning where the var is listed, and `roles/home_assistant/templates/configuration.yaml.j2` carries the constraint comment on the block that misleads. Nothing else was changed — no YAML edit, no restart: a `.storage` object is not reachable from this file. ⛔ **And the standby half has no subject yet:** HD-1109 measured oldsrv running no `home-assistant-standby`/`keepalived` container at all, so "prove the standby edge" is blocked by the cold-deploy leg, not by this row's technique. ✅ **Reconciled 2026-10-19, and the write is owned in IaC** — `roles/home_assistant/tasks/pi.yml`, tag `hd418` — because a `.storage` object is not reachable from the YAML: the leg reads the object and its sha256, refuses a shape it does not understand, reports enforced-vs-rendered, backs the object up to `/var/backups/ha-storage/http.<timestamp>.pre-hd418.json` (dir 0700, file 0600, both hashes printed into the converge log), rewrites `trusted_proxies` in **both** `stable` and `pending` from `ha_trusted_proxies`, restarts `home-assistant-primary` **alone** (never the co-located keepalived, so the VIP cannot move), waits for `/api/` to answer, then re-reads the object and asserts it. Live on the Pi: before, `stable` = `pending` = the two-entry list (the `traefik-public` edge /32 + the Pi's own host /32), sha256 `5f1e472d5…`, against the rendered three (that pair **plus** `oldsrv_home_ip`/32) → after, both blocks carry the rendered three (sha256 `30c99203e…`), `pending.error` still `not_promoted` and `yaml_migration_done` preserved; `--check` `ok=27 changed=1`, the detached apply `ok=32 changed=3 failed=0`, the same apply again `ok=26 changed=0` (no second restart), HA `Up`, the VIP still on the Pi, `https://ha.kogler.si/` → 200. **Behaviour, not config text** — HA's own log after the restart: `[http.ban] Login attempt or request with invalid authentication from <the driving laptop's Home address>` for a request taken through the Pi's `traefik-ha` edge (the REAL client IP, so an XFF from a trusted peer is honoured), and `[http.forwarded] Received X-Forwarded-For header from an untrusted proxy <that same address>` + `400 Bad Request` for a forged XFF sent straight to `VIP:8123` — a non-trusted source is not treated as spoofable. ⚠ **What is NOT proven:** the takeover-shaped request was never re-sent FROM the oldsrv edge — that host is another lane's to run commands on — so the `oldsrv_home_ip`/32 entry rests on list equality + the two behaviours above + HA's pre-change `untrusted proxy <oldsrv_home_ip>` line at 09:11 the same morning, not on a post-change 200 from that socket. The `standby` half stays void: HD-1109 measures the standby UNDEPLOYED, so there is no second instance to reconcile and nothing to probe. ✅ The sentence in `templates/docker_services/traefik-internal/dynamic/routes.yml.j2` that told the reader to "read the rendered `configuration.yaml`" now names the storage object, points at the `hd418` leg that owns the write, and records that the XFF-present → 200 read **through that middleware** is still owed. ✅ **Fixed 2026-10-19 (owner authorised the overnight pass), and the defect was never on the Pi:** `ha-cert-sync.service` failed every tick with `rsync: [sender] send_files failed to open /opt/traefik/certs/kogler.si-key.pem: Permission denied (13)` → exit 23 because **the issuer's key files are `0600 root:root` while both pullers rsync as `ansible-admin`** — oldsrv's `traefik-cert-pull` fails with the identical line, so this is one mode on the VPS, not two broken consumers (the Pi's unit runs as root with the right key: no `User=`, no scope or Split-Access-Mode refusal, no wrong user). rsync hid it behind its own fast path — a sender opens only the files it has to send — so the mode surfaced at the 2026-10-17 10:08 renewal. Repaired at the file level on the issuer (**0640 `root:ansible-admin`**) and owned in IaC in `roles/docker_services`. Proof: the unit's own `ExecStart` command exits 0 by hand, four consecutive ticks `Finished` after `reset-failed`, `systemctl --failed` no longer lists it, and the pulled key now hashes to the cert it ships. ⚠ It was **not** a TLS outage — the renewal reused the key, so every consumer kept serving valid TLS with a 100 % broken sync; the hazard is the next key-rotating renewal (new cert lands, new key fails → a mismatched pair on every edge). The watcher §Certification named as missing is in too: `homelab_unit_last_success_timestamp_seconds` + the `unit-last-success-stale` rule — and the read that justified it is that the RESULT half had been sitting on `0`/`failed` in the store for two days, so the gap was an eye on the age, not a new signal. A v6-preferring client **at home or on the tailnet therefore gets no AAAA at all**, which is a split-horizon shape question, not a firewall verdict. ⏳ **Read it in this order:** (1) `Resolve-DnsName vps.kogler.si -Type AAAA` against the laptop’s resolver AND against `1.1.1.1` — the two tiers differ on purpose now, and HD-1131 is the precedent for an internal tier answering from a stale row; (2) bypass DNS from the same v6 source: `curl -6v --max-time 10 --resolve vps.kogler.si:443:2a0a:4cc0:60:fcc:d820:9dff:fe4f:95f5 https://vps.kogler.si` — the only read here that isolates reachability from naming; (3) the VPS and router counters with the counter-only `ip6 nexthdr icmpv6 counter` discriminator beside `nstat Icmp6InNeighborAdvertisements`, never route presence. ✅ **Ruled 2026-10-12: yes, but sequenced AFTER the publish proves out**, so no client is ever handed an AAAA that answers nothing. The mechanism is not free: `dns_primary_ipv6` exists at `IaC/ansible/group_vars/all/main.yml:419` but is read only by the Cloudflare path, so adding it internally is a `zone_kogler_si` schema change and belongs to HD-436’s file, not to a firewall commit. ✅ **Both owner verdicts given 2026-10-12:** the `tcp dport 22/443`, `udp dport 51820` and RustDesk accepts are family-agnostic in an `inet` table, so HD-460 landing makes them reachable over v6 — **ruled: KEEP THE PARITY** — the accepts are family-agnostic and are NOT scoped `meta nfproto ipv4`, which moves the duty onto the proof: the lane shows fail2ban/CrowdSec logging a real client address over v6 (an address hidden by NAT66 reopens this call). ⚠ **The read was taken 2026-10-13 (`ssh vps`, read-only) and it splits:** the `:22` half is **discharged** — `sshd` listens on `[::]:22`, the accepts are family-agnostic (`tcp dport 22 accept`, no `nfproto`), and the journal the sshd jail matches carries **196** lines from a real client address in the advertised home /64 (`Connection from 2a00:ee2:2700:8f00:… on 2a0a:4cc0:60:fcc:d820:9dff:fe4f:95f5 port 22`, 62 `Accepted publickey`, journal window 2026-08-22→2026-10-05). The `:443` half **cannot be taken yet**: nothing binds `[::]:443` (`0.0.0.0:80`/`0.0.0.0:443` + the tunnel-bound `:4443`) and the Traefik access log holds **no** v6 client address across 2026-09-03→2026-10-13. The gap is now **stated where a rebuild would look for it** — a comment on the `laptop-domen` row in `group_vars/all/main.yml` and §The laptop alias contract — and `docs/network-addresses-generated.md` was re-rendered with `scripts/render_all.py` (drift-free: the render is unchanged, because no SSOT *value* moved; only prose moved). ⛔ **The owner call stays parked:** a `network_static_hosts` row + a find-guarded `rb4011_*_delta` in the Pi's shape, or no reservation at all with the alias contract resting on the name. |

So the leg is not one line from working; it is two, and both are invisible to `--syntax-check` and to every offline gate because the block is gated `not ansible_check_mode`. **(2) The candidate build uses syntax Jinja does not have:** the `set_fact` builds `_tech_prune_candidates` with a list comprehension (`{{ [ {...} for r in … for rec in … if … ] }}`). Jinja has no comprehension in expression position — `{{ [x for x in [1,2]] }}` is a `TemplateSyntaxError: expected token ',', got 'for'` under the venv's jinja2 3.1.6 — so the task dies at render even after the read is fixed. It parses, it passes `--syntax-check`, and it passes every offline gate, because the block is gated `not ansible_check_mode` and nothing else evaluates it: a leg can sit broken in the tree with every gate green. **Nothing was deleted and nothing was changed by this discovery** — both failures are before any delete, and the read that did answer (body shape, one retired name) reported zero A records, which matches the row's own `NXDOMAIN` read-back. **Left in place untouched:** the shipped shape and the comprehension, because repairing them switches on a never-run delete leg against live DNS, which this row's owner-present framing reserves. The row's ⏳ stands; whoever takes it fixes both defects, then the first real run should legitimately name zero candidates. |

| HD-1160 | 2 | owner | 2 | **A per-policy DNS upstream has nowhere to live: Technitium exposes no per-client-group upstream surface (on the pin and upstream), and every router `:53` policy currently aims at ONE shared resolver address — so Kids → Cloudflare Families and IoT → Quad9 cannot be built as ruled** — this is the wall HD-480/HD-481/HD-482 run into, and it is a product/architecture decision, not an unbuilt feature. **Measured 2026-10-18 on `technitium/dns-server:15.6.0`** (`settings/get` → `version: "15.6"`): `api/settings/client/options/{get,set}`, `client/resolvers/{get,set}`, `client/networks/get`, `client/customOptions/get`, `client/accessRules/*`, `clientOptions/list`, `clientSpecificResolvers/get` all **404**, while `zones/permissions/get` answers `200` in the same probe; the console JS the container serves calls no such endpoint; upstream `APIDOCS.md` documents none (its `Group` entries are admin groups and the retired Advanced Blocking **app**, and `/api/apps/list` → `installed: []`). **And the live router has no spare lever:** a read-only `ip firewall nat` print holds one `:53` pair — `tablet-ipad` → `dns_primary_ip`, the same address the Home tier and this operator's tailnet traffic resolve through. A Families or Quad9 upstream set there is a FLEET-WIDE upstream change, which is exactly why [network-rejected.md](docs/network-rejected.md) declines "point DHCP at the router's global `/ip dns`". **Prior art, so this is not re-litigated:** one global resolver cannot differentiate per-VLAN upstreams, and Kids filtering scoped to VLAN 40 only was rejected because the tablets sit on Home VLAN — per-MAC dst-nat was the answer, and that mechanism needs a per-policy resolver ADDRESS that does not exist ([network-rejected.md](docs/network-rejected.md); HD-326; HD-1133; the parked rows in HD-1142). **The options, as facts:** **(A)** one extra Technitium instance or listener per policy, each carrying one upstream, with the router dst-nat aiming at the address — costs a fourth stateful DNS instance (seed surface, HD-436-class parity, `dns-seed.timer`, the drift gates) and an address to publish; **(B)** restore/extend the router `:53` rows onto such addresses — depends on A, and blocked by the VLAN-99 trunk parity fix in HD-1142 before any router write is safe; **(C)** collapse to one upstream for the whole tier and drop the per-policy claim — cheapest, and it re-writes the §Tier policy rulings the owner ruled 2026-10-08; **(D)** filter at a component that can do per-client policy (a Pi-hole-class external blocker is rejected in [services-rejected.md](docs/services-rejected.md), and the app store offers nothing here). ⛔ **What must not happen by default:** shipping a second resolver instance as a side effect of a DNS row, or a router write, from an unattended session. ⏳ **Owner:** choose A/B/C/D. The applied half needs no decision and is already IaC (the tuning dict, the block-list posture and the asserted read-back landed from this lane). · [network-dns.md](docs/network-dns.md) §What the pin can and cannot enforce · [network-rejected.md](docs/network-rejected.md) · [services-rejected.md](docs/services-rejected.md) |

What a snapshot story is proved by, and the naming rule, now live in [storage.md](docs/storage.md) §Snapshot & Replication; the unit's prose left the shipped payload with it. ⏳ **This row cannot be deleted yet:** three root brief references still name it (`prompt.md`, `prompt-storage-backup.md` ×2) and `check_todo_done --refs` fails a brief that points at a non-existent row — the re-derive is orchestrator work, not lane work. ⏳ ⚠ **Both causes on this row are void as written — measured 2026-10-19.** `storage-syncoid.service` sits in `activating (start)` since **2026-10-18 06:45:04** (18 h 43 m, `NRestarts=0`, `JobTimeoutUSec=infinity`), and **no `zfs receive` is running**: the unit's cgroup holds 5 tasks (`bash` → `syncoid` → its tee helper → `sh -c "zfs send -I tank/data/services@syncoid_nas_2026-10-08:21:00:08 …@2026-10-18:06:45:05 \| mbuffer \| pv -s 23712 \| zfs receive -s -F bulk/data/services"` → `pv`), and `zfs send`/`mbuffer`/`zfs receive` are gone from the process table (`receive_resume_token` empty). The wedge is **`pv`**: `State: R`, unit CPU 18 h 41 m against 18 h 43 m wall, `/proc/<pv>/io` frozen at `rchar 4980 wchar 0 syscw 0` across two reads — it is spinning on a pipe whose reader exited, so `sh` never reaps, `syncoid` never returns, and the oneshot unit never finishes. The stream was 23 KB. Retention: `hourly` is **43** on `tank/data/services`, **24** (at window) on `tank/data/db-dumps` and on both `bulk/data/*` replicas — not ~100; the "~100" was prose, and sanoid keeps no `sanoid.log` here, so the deferral line exists only in a manual `sanoid --prune-snapshots --readonly --verbose` run. Full read + the probe set: [docs/storage.md](docs/storage.md) §Reading the activating state. ✅ **The parked reap was run 2026-10-19 (owner act, authorised):** `systemctl stop storage-syncoid.service` at 12:58:49 killed the tree (`Main process exited, code=killed, status=15/TERM`, `Consumed 1d 6h 13min CPU`). **Then the authorised re-fire became the finding.** systemd re-triggers a stopped triggered unit in the same second; the send re-ran 42 KB, and the receiver answered `cannot restore to bulk/data/services@autosnap_2026-10-08_21:05:07_monthly: destination already exists` — twice, identically — leaving the same wedged `pv`. The cause is structural, not a stuck stream: **66 snapshots on the destination are newer than the send base and every one is a sanoid `@autosnap_*` the replica takes for itself** (`use_template = data` on `bulk/data/*`), and an incremental `-F` receive cannot land on a target holding snapshots the stream does not contain. So "the replica retains independent snapshot history" and "the replica is a syncoid target" are mutually exclusive on this dataset, and this replication leg has been dead since the base snapshot — 2026-10-08 21:00, ten days. Pruning cannot resume for the same reason it never did: sanoid's `iszfsbusy()` greps `ps -Ao args=` for a `zfs send`/`zfs receive` line naming the dataset, and the wedged pipeline's **shell wrapper** keeps exactly that string in the process table forever — so both datasets read `currently in zfs send or receive` while no zfs process exists at all (`sanoid --prune-snapshots --readonly --verbose` defers every one of 55 hourly against an `hourly = 24` window). ⛔ **Parked, one line wider:** letting one receive land means destroying the destination's post-base history — `zfs destroy bulk/data/services@autosnap_2026-10-08_21:05:07_monthly` plus 65 more, i.e. the replica's own rollback history on `bulk` — or removing the replica datasets from `sanoid.conf.j2` and letting those 66 age out. Neither is an overnight act. | `systemctl show sanoid.service -p Result,ExecMainStatus` = success/0 + fresh `@autosnap_*_hourly` on the configured datasets | ✅ reaped + re-fired 2026-10-19 — ⏳ the 66 destination snapshots (owner) |

| HD-440 | 2 | AI | 1 | **Every `docker_services` converge restarts a healthy service: the HD-268c stub-dir guard deletes the rendered file it exists to protect** — minted by the closed runner/cockpit lane session 2026-09-23, found by running HD-386's converge **twice**. `roles/docker_services/tasks/deploy-service.yml:82` pre-removes `{{ docker_compose_dir }}/{{ svc.name }}/<extra>` with `state: absent` before templating, to clear the empty **directory** docker compose auto-creates for a missing bind-mount source — the task's own comment says "pre-remove any such *stub*". But `state: absent` does not care that the path is a regular file: on oldsrv `/opt/lan-litellm/config.yaml` is a regular file (`0644 root:root`), so **every converge deletes it**, `Template extra .j2 files` recreates it (⇒ `changed`), and the `extra_templates_result` guard then restarts the stack "to apply changed extra config". Measured, same commit twice: `--tags docker_services,lan-litellm` → `ok=83 changed=5 failed=0`, and again → `ok=83 changed=5 failed=0`, with `lan-litellm` reading `Up 2 minutes` then `Up 3 minutes`. A live LLM gateway is restarted for no reason on every converge, indefinitely, and no run ever reports a failure. ⏳ **Fix is a type test, not a heuristic:** `stat` the path and remove it only when it is a **directory**, then let the template task's own checksum comparison decide — and prove it the way this was found: two converges from one commit, the second must be `changed=0`. ⏳ Audit the other repeat in the same pass: `nut : Clear stray ACL on upssched-cmd and restore mode 0750` also reported `changed` on both runs, so it is very likely the same class (an unconditional `setfacl`/`mode` fix instead of a converge-gated one). ✅ **VPS residue audited and the guilty task fixed 2026-09-27** (found by taking HD-452's same-commit-twice measurement, which is the audit this clause asked for). Now gated on the two templates it is the only consumer of (services.yaml / widgets.yaml). (2) **`Docker compose up -d for renovate` is honest and stays**: renovate's project has ONE service and it is an exit-0 one-shot (measured: `renovate exited(0) 10 minutes ago`), so `state: present` starts it again every converge and the module reports that truthfully — the converge IS renovate's trigger, and re-scheduling that is an owner-visible behaviour change, so it is recorded here rather than silently re-timed. ⚠ Also measured on the way: `--tags docker_services,<svc>` is NOT surgical — the role-level tag `docker_services` is inherited by every task in the role, so the 'surgical' form documented at deploy-service.yml:183 (and used by HD-440's own evidence, `ok=83`) runs the WHOLE role; the real per-service scope is `--tags <svc>` alone. ✅ **FIXED + PROVEN 2026-09-23 (the spark-edge lane, on spark):** the pre-remove is now a `stat` + remove-**only-if-directory** pair, and the `HD-162` compose-validation shell task got `changed_when: false` (it was the whole of the residual churn — a bare `shell` task reports `changed` forever, which is the same reporting class as the stub bug). Proof on spark, same commit twice: `--tags docker_services,spark-dashboard` → `changed=3` then **`changed=0 failed=0`**, no restart handler, `traefik-spark` kept its id and `StartedAt`. ✅ **BOTH PROVEN 2026-09-28 on oldsrv** — the run this row was owed: a full enabled-services converge of ONE commit → `ok=314 changed=10 failed=0`, then the **same commit again** → **`ok=314 changed=0 failed=0`**. (a) `lan-litellm/config.yaml` survived: the stat read `isdir=False isreg=True size=1703`, the stub-removal task **skipped**, the template task reported `ok` (not `changed`), the mtime is still **2026-09-23 00:46:40**, and `Restart lan-litellm stack to apply changed extra config` **skipped** — the live gateway stayed `Up … (healthy)` through both runs. | HD-1101 | 2 | Network | 1 | **The resolver shape guard was oldsrv-shaped, so it silently SKIPPED on the Pi.** HD-484's fail-loud assertion read `nm_oldsrv_profile` (an oldsrv-only host var) to find the profile, so on the Pi — which declares `host_resolver_dns` (`host_vars/pi.kogler.si.yml`) and writes it into a rendered keyfile — the guard never ran at all: a broken `dns_*` chain there reaches NetworkManager as a literal `{{ … }}`, nmcli and a keyfile both accept it, and the box keeps its IP and loses every name it has. ✅ **Fixed, teeth measured 2026-10-19:** one assert in `roles/network/tasks/main.yml` keyed on `host_resolver_dns` alone, plus a per-host `set_fact` naming the profile THIS host actually writes (`nm_oldsrv_profile` → the nmcli leg; `nm_pi_profile` + `nm_parent_iface` → the keyfile leg; neither → empty, refused) and the interface it writes it on — four clauses: comma-joined IPv4 list, profile non-empty, iface non-empty, `nm_resolver_iface == ansible_facts['default_ipv4']['interface']` (the real cross-check on a keyfile host). Tagged `[network, nm-pi, nm-resolver]` so it runs on both apply legs; it gathers `ansible_default_ipv4` itself because `--tags <anything>` skips fact gathering; placed BEFORE the keyfile render so nothing downstream reads an unverified string. The nmcli block's own gather/set_fact pair is gone — the guard now sets `nm_resolver_iface`, which `device reapply` reads. | Per-host shape check keyed on the var each host applies, not on one host's profile var. **Done.** The remaining half is the write leg below. | `--check --tags nm-resolver` on the Pi, both ways, measured at this tip: clean → `ok=22 changed=0 failed=0`; with the committed fixture `scripts/testdata/nm-resolver-shape/broken-dns-tertiary.yml` → `failed=1 rc=2`, `Cannot proceed on pi.kogler.si: host_resolver_dns must be a comma-joined IPv4 list…`, play stopped before any write (`changed=0`, both legs re-run 2026-10-19 on this tree). ⚠ Pass it as `-e @"$PWD/scripts/…"` — `ansible-run.sh` cds to `IaC/ansible`, so a bare relative path is parsed as inventory and the guard is never reached. | ✅ **The Pi half of that owner-window act RAN 2026-10-19** — `--check` first (`ok=22 changed=0 failed=0`), then the real `--tags nm-resolver` apply DETACHED (`setsid nohup` + log `/tmp/converge-pi-nmresolver-apply-20261019-174641.log` + `pgrep` proof; never `--diff`, never behind a timeout): **`ok=22 changed=0 failed=0`**, and the guard executed live on this box for the first time and PASSED (resolver = the `dns_tertiary_ip`,`dns_secondary_ip` pair, profile `pi-eth0`, iface `eth0` — the default-route interface, which is the cross-check that matters on a keyfile host). All three reads are unchanged before → after: the rendered keyfile's `dns=` line (the tertiary;secondary pair in `;` form) → the same; `/etc/resolv.conf`'s two `nameserver` lines in that order → the same; `getent hosts ha.kogler.si` → the VIP and `getent hosts github.com` → a public answer → the same, with the split-horizon control `getent hosts llm.kogler.si` → spark's Home address still answering. The box already held the rendered pair, so the apply's entire content was the guard, and the rollback below was never needed. ⚠ **A tag-class finding this row carried wrong:** on a keyfile host `--tags nm-resolver` cannot WRITE the resolver — the keyfile render is tagged `[network, nm-pi]`, and every `nm-resolver` task in the block is gated on `nm_oldsrv_profile`, which the Pi does not define. The guard is co-tagged `nm-pi, nm-resolver` so it runs on either leg (that is what made this apply meaningful), but the Pi's write leg is `nm-pi`, exactly as [network-dns.md](docs/network-dns.md) §Host-side resolver already says. ⚠ **And the `nm-pi` render is not idempotent on this box (measured, deliberately not fixed here):** `--check --tags nm-resolver,nm-pi` reports `changed` on `Render Pi eth0 NetworkManager keyfile` while every FUNCTIONAL line already matches (`id=pi-eth0`, `interface-name=eth0`, `method=manual`, the `address1`/`gateway` pair, `dns=`, `dns-search=`) — the delta is the template's leading comment block, reworded in the tree after the file was last rendered (on-disk mtime 2026-10-08 01:17). A comment-only `changed` on the file that carries this box's addressing is the "a task that can only assert must not report changed" class, it belongs to `roles/network`, and it also fires the `reload NetworkManager` handler for a comment — so this lane left it for that role's own row rather than re-writing an addressing file to fix prose. ⏳ **oldsrv's half is still owed**: it was authored and read with `--check` only here (no oldsrv converge ran in this lane), and its nmcli `device reapply` now depends on the guard's `nm_resolver_iface`, so the oldsrv read-back rides that apply. Rule + teeth + the fixture: [network-dns.md](docs/network-dns.md) §Host-side resolver. ✅ **The rollback for this box's apply, written into this row BEFORE the run:** the Pi's resolver rides a rendered keyfile, so undo is one line and needs no boot and no link drop — `sed -i 's/^dns=.*/dns=<dns_tertiary_ip>;<dns_secondary_ip>;/' /etc/NetworkManager/system-connections/pi-eth0.nmconnection && nmcli connection reload && nmcli device reapply eth0` (substitute the two SSOT addresses named in `group_vars/all/main.yml` → `network-addresses-generated.md`; they are the pair this row's own read above quotes) — and the two reads that must answer again afterwards are `getent hosts github.com` (public) and `getent hosts ha.kogler.si` (split-horizon). ⚠ `dig` and `resolvectl` are BOTH absent on this box, so glibc/`getent` is the resolver instrument here and the rendered keyfile + `/etc/resolv.conf` are the two states to read. |

| HD-1103 | 1 | Pi + HA | 2 | **Logs are not rotating on the Pi — logrotate refuses every file, and the cause is a tmpfs mounted WITHOUT a `mode=` option, not a mode that drifted.** Measured 2026-10-08: `/var/log` is `1777` and logrotate exits 1 on every stanza while the journal keeps working, so the box looks healthy and only `systemctl --failed` says otherwise; the Pi runs HA off microSD, so unrotated logs are a slow path to a full rootfs. ⚠ **Premise corrected 2026-10-19 (measured, and it changes the fix):** `/var/log` here is a **tmpfs** (Pi SD-wear strategy item 3), and a tmpfs mounted without `mode=` simply arrives `1777 root:root` — the MOUNT owns the value, nothing drifted it, and the row's `0755 root:root` target is wrong on the group: what nas and oldsrv measure from the root fs is `755 root:adm`, the fleet value logrotate's secure-parent check accepts. Measured on a scratch tmpfs: `mount -o remount,mode=0755` returns **0 and leaves the directory 1777** — `mode=` is a mount-time option on tmpfs. So neither half alone works: an fstab option is undone-by-nothing but leaves the running mount wrong until a boot (an owner act), and a chmod alone is undone by the next boot. ✅ **Landed 2026-10-19** in `roles/home_assistant/tasks/pi.yml`: the fstab line carries `…size=100m,mode=0755,uid=0,gid=0`; the **applied** mount is read with `findmnt --target` under `check_mode: false`; the directory is `stat`ed and reconciled to `0755 root:adm` only when the measurement disagrees; the mode is asserted when not in check mode. No chmod-only patch, no reboot. Second half, same row: `smartmontools` is measured, not assumed — `roles/ai_diag` probes `smartctl --scan` and start+enables or stop+disable+**mask**s symmetrically, so the exit-17 tenant of `systemctl --failed` clears itself and a host that gains a device gets smartd back without anyone remembering. | Both halves are owned in IaC now (the Pi fstab line + the `ai_diag` probe). Nothing left to hand-fix; the legs have now been run — ✅ `pi_sd_trim` on 2026-10-19 (`--check` `ok=28 changed=0`, detached apply `ok=29 changed=0 failed=0`) plus one real `systemctl start logrotate.service`, which succeeded and rotated; the `ai_diag` leg was NOT run and smartmontools is parked as an owner act, with the reason measured in the notes below. | `logrotate -d /etc/logrotate.conf` re-read on the Pi 2026-10-19 after the apply: **no** `error: skipping … insecure permissions` line at all — only `does not exist -- skipping` for files this host lacks (btmp, wtmp, cloud-init, mail.log, alternatives) and `wtmp.db … log does not need rotating`. `/var/log` measures `755 root:adm` under live options `rw,nosuid,nodev,noexec,noatime,size=102400k tmpfs` — **no `mode=` on the running mount**, exactly the gap the two legs exist for — while `/etc/fstab` line 4 carries `mode=0755,uid=0,gid=0`. `smartctl --scan` as root → empty, rc 0, i.e. the mask decision the leg makes here. | `systemctl --failed` on the Pi: **two units at the start of this pass, one at the end**, and the one that remains is named rather than waved away. ✅ `logrotate.service` — converged the leg that owns it (`--tags pi_sd_trim`: `--check` `ok=28 changed=0`, detached apply `ok=29 changed=0 failed=0`; its report line reads `/var/log` `0755 root:adm` under live options with no `mode=` on the mount — the state the two legs leave until a boot), then **let the unit run for real**: `systemctl start logrotate.service` → rc 0, `Result=success`, `ExecMainStatus=0`, journal `Finished logrotate.service`, and the rotation is on disk — `auth.log.1`, `syslog.1` (75 MB), `kern.log.1`, `user.log.1`, `cron.log.1` all stamped 17:50:21 with `/var/lib/logrotate/status` at the same second. The unit left the `--failed` list because its last result is success. ⚠ Honest about the instrument: the same command issued `systemctl reset-failed logrotate.service` before the start, so the proof is the successful run above, not the reset. ⚠ **Letting it rotate exposed the next defect, and it is not the one this row is about:** `/var/log` is a **100 MB tmpfs** and `/etc/logrotate.conf` is `weekly` + `rotate 4` with **no `compress`** — measured after the run, 77 M of that 100 M is in use, so rotation moved bytes from `/var/log/x` to `/var/log/x.1` on the SAME filesystem. Four uncompressed weekly copies of a ~75 MB/period `syslog` cannot fit in 100 MB, so the failure this row feared (a full `/var/log`, nothing writable) is still ahead of this box — later, and in RAM rather than on the SD. ⏳ **Ask, for the retention owner:** pick the policy a tmpfs can hold — `compress` + `delaycompress`, or `rotate 1` + `maxsize` — and own it in IaC; the repo today owns only the mount and the mode (`roles/home_assistant/tasks/pi.yml`), no logrotate config at all. ✅ **`smartmontools.service` measured to the end, then PARKED as an owner act — no hand-disable, no mask, the unit left exactly as found** (`enabled`/`failed`, `ExecMainStatus=17` = "no devices to monitor", last start 2026-09-02). The class is closed by measurement rather than opinion, root on the box: `smartctl --scan` → empty, rc 0; `smartctl -i -d scamm /dev/mmcblk0` → **`Unknown device type 'scamm'`** (smartmontools **7.4-3**, which is also the apt candidate in trixie — `scamm` needs ≥7.5); `-d removable` is not a smartctl TYPE at all (it is a smartd.conf directive); `-d scsi` → "Both 36 and 64 byte INQUIRY failed [Invalid argument]"; `-d sat` → "Read Device Identity failed"; `smartd -d -q onecheck` on `/dev/mmcblk0 -d removable -H` → "unable to autodetect device type / not available / Unable to monitor any SMART enabled devices. Exiting (17)", on `-d scsi` → exit 16. **No `/etc/smartd.conf` line starts smartd on this box**, so the `ai_diag` probe cannot be made to "enable it for real" by config alone. The wear question that SMART ask was standing in for has no tool-free surface either: `/sys/bus/mmc/devices/mmc0:aaaa/` exposes cid/csd/date/fwrev/manfid/… and **no `life_a`/`life_b`/`pre_eol_info`** — a `find /sys` for those three names returns nothing on 6.18.34+rpt-rpi-v8, because the card is an SD (CID `03534453…`, `name=SN128`) and those attributes are eMMC EXT_CSD fields — and `mmc-utils` is absent, so reading SD health would need a package install, which this lane did not do. ⏳ **The two real choices, for the owner, and nothing else:** (i) a smartmontools ≥7.5 source on this host to buy `-d scamm`, or (ii) the end state `ai_diag` already derives — a host whose `smartctl --scan` is empty declares smartd off — noting that the mask that leg applies is what the next UNTAGGED converge will do **by design**, which is an owner call and not a lane's. ✅ `ha-cert-sync.service` is off this list as of 2026-10-19 — the HD-418-family tick failure was the ISSUER's `0600` key file against an `ansible-admin` rsync, fixed at that file (0640 `root:ansible-admin`) and now owned in `roles/docker_services`; `systemctl --failed` on the Pi lists the two units above and nothing else. The mode becomes truthful on the mount line at the next boot, not from a converge. Durable rules: [observability.md](docs/observability.md) §Pi SD-card wear strategy, [deployment-ansible.md](docs/deployment-ansible.md) §ai_diag. |

| HD-1148 | 2 | AI + owner | 2 | **The Docker engine family is PINNED — `host_apt_pin_vars` in `versions.yml` names the suite and `roles/docker` installs `package=version`, so `apt-mark hold` freezes repo state instead of an accident** — measured in this worktree: `dpkg-query -W -f='${Version}' docker-ce` reads `5:29.8.2-1~debian.13~trixie` on oldsrv, `5:29.7.2-1~debian.13~trixie` on pi and vps, `5:29.6.2-1~ubuntu.24.04~noble` on spark; nas carries no docker-ce at all. Owner ruled option (a), executed across the WHOLE suite, because held-but-unpinned is the same defect five packages wide — containerd.io and the two plugins ship their own upstream trains, not the engine's. ✅ `host_apt_pin_vars` (`group_vars/all/versions.yml`) maps package → the var that pins it: `docker_ce_version` `5:29.8.2` for docker-ce + docker-ce-cli + docker-ce-rootless-extras (ONE source package, so one version), `containerd_io_version` `2.3.6`, `docker_buildx_plugin_version` `0.37.1`, `docker_compose_plugin_version` `5.5.1`, next to `alloy` + `tailscale` — and BOTH `common_apt_hold_packages` (roles/common) and `monitoring_hygiene_pins` (roles/monitoring) are DERIVED from that map, so the held set, the install set and the pin-lag report cannot disagree and no package can be held with no pin. Every value is the highest release ALREADY RUNNING somewhere in the fleet, not the newest upstream release: an upgrade or a no-op on every host and never a downgrade (`--allow-downgrades` stays forbidden — HD-1094's rule). The pin states the dpkg UPSTREAM version, epoch included, never a deb string: download.docker.com stamps the same release as `-1~debian.13~trixie` and `-1~ubuntu.24.04~noble`, so spark's Ubuntu base is exactly why the fleet pins a FAMILY — `roles/docker` installs `pkg=<pin>-*` (the apt module's version wildcard, hyphen-anchored so `5:29.8.2-*` cannot match a `5:29.8.20-*`) and no distro suffix is written anywhere in the repo, which is also why there is no per-host override to read. ✅ Solver-proved on vps + pi + spark across both suites: every component resolves everywhere (`Inst docker-ce [5:29.6.2-1~ubuntu.24.04~noble] (5:29.8.2-1~ubuntu.24.04~noble Docker CE:noble [arm64])`), so no pin had to be lowered and no host got its own value. ✅ A pin bump needs the hold LIFTED, not a flag: the module path is blocked by the hold. Measured on vps with the six specs this role builds, `apt-get -s -y install 'docker-ce=5:29.8.2-*' …` prints "5 upgraded" and dies with `E: Held packages were changed and -y was used without --allow-change-held-packages` (rc 100), while the same command WITHOUT `-y` resolves the plan (`Inst docker-ce [5:29.7.2-…] (5:29.8.2-…)`); a bare `apt-get -y install docker-ce` behaves the same way. The apt module always passes `-y` — so every `--check --tags docker` on a held host aborted at the install task, and a live apply would have too. `roles/docker` now lifts the hold for the suite names that are held AND off their pin, installs in the same block, and re-holds in `always` with an assert against `apt-mark showhold`: a failed install leaves the pre-state (held, old version), and a host already at its pin lifts nothing. ⛔ `allow_change_held_packages` / `--allow-change-held-packages` stays out of the fleet — the exception names its packages in the task list instead of switching the fence off for a whole apt call; the blanket `apt-mark unhold $(apt-mark showhold)` is worse than the bug (it would hand `alloy` and `tailscale` to any `apt upgrade` racing the converge). ✅ `docker-ce-rootless-extras` is now IN that suite list — the engine's own rootless support, one source package with the engine so `docker_ce_version` pins all three — and one list now drives the apt call, the hold decision and the read-back, so a pinned docker-family package cannot sit outside the install set: an assert fails the converge when `host_apt_pin_vars` names a package pinned by a download.docker.com var that `docker_ce_suite_packages` does not install (mutation-proved: pull the name from the list, or add a fake `docker-screenshot-plugin` to the map, and the run reddens before it installs anything). Host effect: vps + pi move it to the pin with the family; spark INSTALLS it (`5 upgraded, 1 newly installed`; deps `dbus-user-session` + `libc6`, both already present) — an install on a host that never carried it, which is why it is named here rather than discovered in the window; nas runs no docker role at all (`playbooks/storage.yml`), so nothing there changes. ✅ `roles/docker` installs the suite in ONE apt call (so a half-moved suite fails in the solver) plus a read-back assert, because the apt module reports OK when an INSTALLED package's requested version is absent from its suite — the silent-skip half of the class; the assert compares the installed dpkg-upstream version to the pin (equality: ahead and behind are both drift) and closes it. ⚠ The install/read/assert tasks carry `[docker, base]`: the old untagged "Install Docker components" task was silently SKIPPED by `--tags docker`. ⚠ **SUPERSEDED — the vps/pi/spark triple in this sentence is the pre-lift shape; the dated pair in the ⏳ tail below is the reading to use.** The sentence this note replaces recorded vps `changed=1 failed=0`, pi `changed=1 failed=0`, spark `changed=1 failed=0`, and it was written into the row by `26f69db8` (2026-10-13) — a tree in which `roles/docker` knew nothing about lifting a hold and the read-back assert stood down under `--check` (`when: not ansible_check_mode`), so the dry run reached the apt task and its `--simulate` reported a would-upgrade. Two things landed between that sentence and today. **(a) The hold became LIVE:** `roles/common/tasks/apt-holds.yml` (`b78c1027`, 2026-10-13) is now read back on the boxes — `apt-mark showhold` names the whole docker suite on vps and pi while `dpkg-query -W -f='${Version}' docker-ce` is still `5:29.7.2-1~debian.13~trixie` against the `5:29.8.2` pin — and a HELD suite is exactly the case that made the pre-lift apt call abort with `failed=1` (measured above), which the older triple did not hit: its `failed=0` proves those hosts were not held yet when it was taken. **(b) `e9517124` (2026-10-17) taught `roles/docker` that case:** under `--check` the lift is not performed, so the role prints what the dry run cannot simulate and the apt task is skipped (`IaC/ansible/roles/docker/tasks/main.yml:195` and `:211`) instead of would-changing or refusing. Both facts together are why the identical command reads `changed=0` on vps and pi now. The owed apply is unchanged by any of this. Unmoved by the supersession: nas `changed=0` with **zero** docker tasks (storage.yml carries no docker role — the no-op is structural, not a skipped task), and oldsrv is UNREACHABLE from this seat (its `ansible-admin` key is refused, pre-existing) and owes no apply: its installed suite already equals the pin. ⏳ **Left: the live apply, and every one of them RESTARTS THE DAEMON and therefore every container on that host — a maintenance action, one host at a time, detached (`nohup … &` + log, then poll), `--check` first, never behind a timeout. ⛔ not overnight.** ✅ **vps DONE 2026-10-18** — `--tags docker` apply `ok=34 changed=3 failed=0`; whole docker family moved to the pin and re-held in the same play (read-back: docker-ce `5:29.8.2-1~debian.13~trixie`, containerd.io `2.3.6-1~debian.13~trixie`, buildx `0.37.1-…`, compose `5.5.1-…`; `apt-mark showhold` names the suite; collector re-reads `match` for all six from the host itself). ⚠ **Incident during the leg, recovered with the owner in the loop:** the dockerd restart stale-socketed the live-restore survivors — traefik held the OLD `/var/run/docker.sock` inode, its docker provider spammed `Cannot connect to the Docker daemon` (0 before 04:19:20, 482 after), and every docker-labeled route 404 duly externally (sso/git/vpn/ai) while authentik stayed healthy internally; headscale — recreated by compose-ensure at 04:20 — crash-looped on OIDC issuance discovery 404 (RestartCount growing; zero headscale restarts in the 12h before, so the apply caused it, not a pre-existing fault). Recovery (owner-approved, container restarts only): `sudo docker restart traefik dozzle crowdsec`, then all four routes answered 200|302, headscale served `/health` 200 and stabilized, traefik provider errors post-restart = 0. The same stale-socket recovery is procedural in deployment-manual.md §1.10 — the pi and spark applies will hit the same shape and need the same `docker restart` of the socket-mounting containers (found with the inspect loop in §1.10) after the daemon restart. ⏳ **Next, pi (PARKED — this lane owns the VPS converge slot only; the pi leg is the l3-pi lane's window, do NOT run it from here):** `nohup bash scripts/ansible/ansible-run.sh playbooks/raspberry_pi.yml --limit pi.kogler.si --tags docker > /tmp/leg-hd1148-pi.log 2>&1 &` (HA + Technitium go with the daemon), → poll; read-back: `ssh pi "dpkg-query -W -f='${Version}' docker-ce"` equals the `docker_ce_version` pin, `ssh pi 'apt-mark showhold'` still lists the suite, `ssh pi 'docker ps --format "{{.Names}} {{.Status}}"'` healthy with no RestartCount growth — and then the §1.10 stale-socket check + survivor restart when the daemon restarts. ✅ **spark DONE 2026-10-18 (owner window, this lane)** — `--tags docker` apply `ok=28 changed=3 failed=0`; the whole family moved to the pin on the box that never held it and re-held in the same play. Read-back: docker-ce `5:29.8.2-1~ubuntu.24.04~noble`, containerd.io `2.3.6-1~ubuntu.24.04~noble`, buildx `0.37.1-…`, compose `5.5.1-…`; `apt-mark showhold` names the suite + alloy. The daemon restart recreated every container (expected) and the engine re-booted the model (~11 min): llm/health back to 200, `restarts=0` on all four containers; the only docker.sock-mounter on the host is dozzle-agent, restarted per §1.10 (the spark-ai vLLM compose mounts no socket). oldsrv owes nothing (no docker role in storage.yml). Rollback = move the four pins back in `versions.yml` and converge; never `--allow-downgrades` on the host. Re-taken read-only from this seat on 2026-10-17, and this is the pair that supersedes the `changed=1` triple above: vps `ok=30 changed=0 failed=0`, pi `ok=31 changed=0 failed=0`, each printing the role's own `--check leaves docker-ce, docker-ce-cli, docker-ce-rootless-extras, containerd.io, docker-buildx-plugin, docker-compose-plugin held, so the apt task below is skipped` line — scoping, not a verdict. spark was not re-taken (this run's seat rule barred the engine slot, and nothing here touched it), so its `changed=1` stands un-re-measured since `e9517124`; the leg that turns it into a verdict is the same `--check --tags docker --limit spark.kogler.si` inside the owner's window. **vps: `bash scripts/ansible/ansible-run.sh playbooks/vps.yml --limit vps.kogler.si --tags docker` · pi: `bash scripts/ansible/ansible-run.sh playbooks/raspberry_pi.yml --limit pi.kogler.si --tags docker` (HA + Technitium go with the daemon) · spark: `bash scripts/ansible/ansible-run.sh playbooks/spark.yml --limit spark.kogler.si --tags docker` — ✅ **spark DONE 2026-10-18** — docker family applied at the pin (`ok=28 changed=3`), engine re-booted and healthy (llm/health 200, restarts=0), §1.10 stale-socket recovery applied. oldsrv owes nothing. Rollback = move the four pins back in `versions.yml` and converge; never `--allow-downgrades` on the host. ⛔ Not by dropping a package from `host_apt_pin_vars` / `common_apt_hold_packages`: an unheld engine moves on the next `apt upgrade`, which is the running-vs-pinned class HD-1094 exists to prevent. ⚠ Read before re-taking it: `apt-mark showhold` is now LIVE on all five hosts (the `roles/common/tasks/apt-holds.yml` leg, read back 2026-10-17), so an apply here moves packages the box is holding — `roles/docker` lifts that hold for the suite names only and puts it back in the same play, which needs no manual `apt-mark unhold` dance and no `--allow-change-held-packages`, and the pre-apply `--check --tags docker` now reads `changed=0` on vps and pi (measured 2026-10-17; spark reads the same path when it is re-checked — under `--check` the lift is not performed, so the apt task says so and skips instead of reporting a hold refusal the real run will not have). · [deployment-compose.md](docs/deployment-compose.md) · [deployment-ansible.md](docs/deployment-ansible.md) §common · [observability.md](docs/observability.md) §Silent-failure hygiene |
| HD-1118 | 2 | AI | 2 | **`scripts/` is foldered — `lib/ gate/ ansible/ secrets/ git/ harness/ bench/ hardware/ testdata/`, with the two umbrella entry points `validate-all.sh` + `render_all.py` at root — and repo-root resolution is structural, so no script assumes a depth.** Landed 2026-10-12 by **re-deriving the 2026-10-09 lane at the tip**: its 13 commits were not replayable (0/13 applied across 676 commits of drift), so that tree survives only as `rescue/hd1118-20261011`. Tree + per-file placement + the registry rule: [scripts/README.md](scripts/README.md); seat planes and what the move means for them: [docs/harness-pi-seats.md](docs/harness-pi-seats.md) §1, [docs/harness-pi-seats.md](docs/harness-pi-seats.md) §5. The tree itself is held by `gate/check_script_layout.py`, a `validate-all.sh` item. ⏳ **Three live legs:** (a) `bash scripts/harness/pi-seat-sync.sh --check` on each seat once it has fast-forwarded — every plane script now deploys from the foldered tree; (b) `install-pi-debian.sh --check` on a clean Debian seat — the only proof the from-zero path converges the moved layout; (c) `bash scripts/harness/install-tmux-conf.sh --push` on the **win11** seat — the provenance marker names the SSOT file (`pi-agent/tmux/tmux.conf`) instead of the installer path, so an installed `~/.tmux.conf` reads `DRIFT` until one push (recognised by `MARKER_LEGACY`, never refused); Fixed by splitting the two jobs — the COMPARE ignores `_note`, the WRITE still renders the whole block, so a push refreshes the prose — and `--self-test` grew four arms whose teeth were checked by mutation: deleting the exclusion reddens the note arm, widening it reddens key-drift, note-masking and unknown-key. | HD-1149 | 1 | AI + gate | 2 | **A filtered converge can report `failed=0` while executing a fraction of a role's tasks — the tag-coverage rule for dynamic includes is enforced nowhere.** Measured on ansible-core 2.21.5 (minimal lab + two live `--check` runs): a tag on a playbook `roles:` entry DOES cascade through `include_tasks` into the included file, but a `tags:` on the include task itself only gates whether the file loads and NEVER cascades into it, and an untagged dynamic include is never expanded by a filtered run even when its children carry the tag; `import_tasks` inherits normally — its children take the import’s tags, so tagging them is unnecessary — and the only playbook-side imports are the `playbooks/tasks/self-converge-guard.yml` legs, whose tasks carry `always`. Two live proofs from this sweep: `--check --tags storage --limit nas.kogler.si` executed 21 of the 36 storage TASK lines (the whole ZFS core — pool import, media uid/gid, dataset properties, `zfs mount -a` — never ran, because `playbooks/storage.yml` pulls the role as a BARE role and the include line carried the tag alone), and `--check --tags technitium_seed --limit vps.kogler.si` ran 26 TASK lines with zero of the five dns-seed units, `changed=0 failed=0`, while the seed self-heal timer was never deployed. Both halves are now tagged (the roles' own schemes: `storage` on the zfs_common/main legs, `[docker_services, technitium_seed]` on dns-seed, `[docker_services, kopia_server, kopia_fingerprint]` on the two kopia-fingerprint tasks that were reachable only as a block); `roles/storage/tasks/main.yml` and `roles/docker_services/tasks/dns-seed.yml` carry the rule at the site. Nothing re-checks it, so the next untagged include re-opens it silently. ⏳ **The gate is authored and self-tested and the live tree is now clean under it; register it in `scripts/validate-all.sh` after owner review** — the `item_q` line is deliberately absent, so the suite stays green without it and the owner's checkpoint is the one thing this row still owes. The two lines to add to the `scripts/gate` block, next to the `check_iac_identifiers.py` pair: `item_q "check_tag_coverage.py (HD-1149: a task below a dynamic include must be selectable)" $PY scripts/gate/check_tag_coverage.py` and `item_q "check_tag_coverage.py --self-test (the four cascade rules, planted)" $PY scripts/gate/check_tag_coverage.py --self-test`. Registering `scripts/testdata/check_tag_coverage/run.sh` can go with them — it is green on its own (8 legs, SKIPs where `ansible-playbook` is not functional). `scripts/gate/check_tag_coverage.py` resolves, for every dynamic `include_tasks`/`include_role` under `IaC/ansible/roles/**`, the tag set that can reach it (its own `tags:` ∪ the `tags:` of the play `roles:` entries that pull its role ∪ `always`), threads blocks and static imports as parse-time inheritance, and reports `A DEAD INCLUDE` (rule 3), `B TAG NEVER LOADS THE FILE` (rules 2/3) and `C UNTAGGED CHILD` (rule 2) over the union of the paths that reach a task. Its four rules are measured, not recalled: `scripts/testdata/check_tag_coverage/run.sh` asks ansible-playbook itself in 8 `debug`-only localhost legs, and `--self-test` plants each rule in a temp tree (11 shapes, exit 2 for a missing tree). **What the live tree reported at authoring time — 2 A, 56 B, 1 C over 49 dynamic includes — is fixed since, tag-only, and the tally reads `A dead includes 0 · B unreachable tags 0 · C untagged children 0`.** A `roles/common/tasks/main.yml:2 include_tasks: system.yml` carries no tag and `storage.yml`/`home_servers.yml`/`raspberry_pi.yml`/`spark.yml` pull `common` bare, so its four `always` tasks and its two untagged ones run in NO filtered run (`vps.yml` tags the entry, so the file loads there — rule 1). A `roles/router/tasks/main.yml:1421 include_tasks: capsman.yml` likewise, with the row's tripwire as its only content: the `routeros_capsman_implemented` guard assert is itself untagged (the C finding at `roles/router/tasks/capsman.yml:21`), so no `--tags` run can ever fire it. The 56 B findings are all the same shape — a child names a tag no include on its path carries, so it runs only under a compound filter: `roles/home_assistant/tasks/pi.yml` (21 tasks) and `standby.yml` (25) tagged `ha_render`/`ha_failover`/`lovelace`/`knx` under includes tagged only `[home_assistant]`; `roles/docker_services/tasks/prepass-authentik.yml` (4) and `run-authentik-glue.yml` (1) tagged `[authentik, secret-egress]` under an include tagged only `docker_services`; `roles/storage/tasks/samba.yml:229-266` (5) tagged `auth-glue` under an include tagged `[samba, storage]`. The fix at each site is the one `f9734124` and `7b37bec3` applied: put the child's tag on the include line too. Applied that way at all five sites: `[common, base]` on the `system.yml` include and on its two untagged legs — the four `tags: always` guards deliberately keep `always` and nothing else, because naming them `common` would let `--skip-tags common` disarm the run-user tripwire; `[network, router-capsman]` on the `capsman.yml` include and on its guard assert, so the guard now travels with the file instead of standing down; the child tag sets on the two HA includes, which keeps both the bare `--tags ha_failover` and the compound `--tags home_assistant,ha_failover` form working (`pi.yml` also picks up `ha_recorder`/`pi_sd_trim`/`hd19`/`hd185`/`hd204`, `standby.yml` `lovelace`/`knx`/`hd17`); `auth-glue` added to the `samba.yml` include; `[docker_services, authentik, secret-egress]` on the two Authentik includes. No `when:`, `notify:`, handler or task logic moved, so an unfiltered run does exactly what it did before — proven on the Pi, where `--check --tags ha_failover --limit pi.kogler.si` went from 1 `home_assistant` TASK line (`ok=19 changed=0`, a green no-op) to 14 (`ok=31`, the failover legs present), and on the NAS, where `--check --tags common --limit nas.kogler.si` now shows the `Sistemska priprava` include and the run-user guard it carries. The rule-1 shape is alive and green where it is done — `roles/cockpit/tasks/maint-user.yml` carries no tag at all and is reached by an untagged include, yet every task in it is selectable, because both playbooks that pull `cockpit` tag the entry. The two doc lines this fix left stale are now corrected in place: the tag section of [docs/deployment-ansible.md](docs/deployment-ansible.md) states the four cascade rules and names the tags the Authentik pre-pass/glue includes actually carry, and the cert-sync tag arithmetic in [docs/services-traefik.md](docs/services-traefik.md)'s edge-model section explains the Pi reach by the include line naming every child tag, so `--tags ha_failover` alone reaching that pull task is explained rather than asserted. ⏳ **Two audit leftovers, both questions rather than work:** `roles/home_assistant/tasks/main.yml` and `roles/cockpit/tasks/main.yml` reach their sub-files through the playbook `roles:` tag, so a playbook that drops that tag would silently unreach them (measured: the two playbooks that pull `home_assistant` do so BARE, so its Pi/standby sub-files depend on the include tag alone, and `cockpit` is the case where the cascade genuinely covers an untagged include); and `roles/storage/tasks/samba.yml` fails any `--check` at its owner-name assert because the lookup `command` above it is skipped in check mode — a check-mode blind spot, not a tag bug. Prior art, all mechanism-only, no row and no gate: [scripts/README.md](scripts/README.md) §Scoped service deploys need BOTH tags, [docs/deployment-ansible.md](docs/deployment-ansible.md) §Tags & surgical runs. |

| HD-1088 | 2 | AI + gate | 2 | **Music durability — the NAS FLAC master is INSIDE backup scope; the Box is the serving copy.** Ruled: option (a) + push-leg shape B. ✅ **IaC authored (this lane).** *Backup:* the `oldsrv` `kopia-agent` include list gained the music master (`templates/docker_services/kopia-agent/docker-compose.yml.j2`) as a read-only bind of `/mnt/nas/media/media/music` snapshotted by **its own line** behind two guards — a source witness (`/proc/self/mountinfo` names `nas.kogler.si:/bulk/media`, or statfs reports `nfs`) **and** a non-empty floor — because kopia calls an EMPTY tree a successful snapshot and the next prune would retire the last snapshot that held the FLACs; every other source stays oldsrv-local and NAS-independent. *Serving:* `push-music.timer` → `storage-push-music.sh` (roles/storage, root) rsyncs the master to the live Box over **SSH/SFTP `:23`** with a `findmnt` mountpoint assert, the same non-empty floor and `--delete --max-delete=50`, strict host keys, and a far-side file-count read-back (proven locally: empty source aborts, dead host key aborts, `:23` is reachable from oldsrv). Paths/Box facts are one SSOT block (`music_master_*`, `music_box_*`) so the snapshot leg, the push leg and the `\nas\music` drop share cannot drift. ⚠ **Why not sanoid:** `bulk/media` is ONE dataset and the master is a directory inside it, and sanoid names datasets — a row naming that path renders, exits 0 and snapshots nothing (the reserved-slot class, [backup.md](docs/backup.md) §The slot-numbering rule, which now also records that `DB07` is the next free dump slot and that a non-database payload takes none). ⏳ **Arm + prove the Kopia leg** (parked: it restarts a running container — run `ansible-playbook site.yml --tags docker_services -e docker_services_scope=kopia-agent --limit oldsrv.kogler.si` detached, then `docker exec kopia-agent kopia snapshot list /mnt/src/music` shows a snapshot and `kopia policy get /mnt/src/music` shows the retention it inherits). ⏳ **Owner, then arm the serving leg:** register a public key on the live Box (Hetzner console — port 23 is key-auth-only and `Hertzner-SB-Data` holds only a CIFS/WebDAV password), seed it root-only 0600 at `/root/.ssh/livebox_push` + the box host keys at `/etc/storage/box_known_hosts`, set `storage_push_music.enabled: true`, converge `--tags storage --limit oldsrv.kogler.si`, then `systemctl start push-music.service` once. ⏳ The ruling's end-to-end proof, both halves: an album imported by Lidarr appears in Navidrome **with no manual copy**, and a Lidarr move/rename leaves the old path gone from the Box in the same run. · [storage.md](docs/storage.md) §Service ↔ Storage Placement · [services-media.md](docs/services-media.md) §Music Pillar · [backup.md](docs/backup.md) ⏳ **2026-10-19 overnight lane E — the leg now RENDERS on the box; the apply of the armed state is parked.** Premise re-measured first: no `/usr/local/sbin/storage-push-music.sh`, no `push-music.*` units, `push-music.timer` = `not-found`. `--check` storage converge then the real scoped apply (`playbooks/home_servers.yml --limit oldsrv.kogler.si --tags storage`, detached) → **ok=46 changed=2 failed=0**; script + service + timer landed, timer **disabled/inactive** because `storage_push_music.enabled` is still false — nothing is scheduled and nothing has crossed. **The guard was proven before the push**: `findmnt` against a non-NFS path exits 1 refusing to push; raising the floor above the real file count exits 1; with the real mount both pass and execution reaches rsync. ⚠ **The third gate does not do what this row claimed**: `--max-delete` is a CAP, not an abort — measured on rsync 3.5.0 with 100 pending deletions and a limit of 50, **50 entries were deleted**, rsync stopped and exited 25 (`Deletions stopped due to --max-delete limit`). On the old reading an empty source would have been fully deleted before the bound tripped; the docs and the script's own failure hint now carry the measured semantics, and the weight sits on the two asserts that run before any rsync. Second correction: with no identity the script aborts at its OWN identity assert *before* the mount assert, so the live path cannot exercise the mount guard until the key exists. ✅ **The delete bound now has a test in the tree** (AI, this lane): `scripts/testdata/rsync-max-delete/run.sh` builds the hazard from the leg's own flags — fire 1 transfers N files with `--remove-source-files`, so fire 2 sees an existing-but-empty source beside N files on the destination, which is what a dead mount hands it — then proves the denominator (uncapped: all N deleted, rc 0) and the cap (`--max-delete=N/2` → exactly N/2 deleted, N/2 left, rc 25). Registered in `validate-all.sh`; `--self-test` reddens on abort semantics, on a bound that does not hold, on a right count with the wrong rc, and on a denominator arm that never had N pending deletions. ⚠ The two rsync arms SKIP on a seat with no rsync binary (the WSL Debian seat is one) — the shape-parity arm and every canary run there, so the gate never reads green from a missing binary. ⛔ **One owner action gates everything else:** register oldsrv's public key on the live Box (port 23 is key-auth-only), seed `/root/.ssh/livebox_push` 0600 + `/etc/storage/box_known_hosts`, set `storage_push_music.enabled: true`, converge `--tags storage --limit oldsrv.kogler.si`, `systemctl start push-music.service` once — until then the two end-to-end proofs (album → Navidrome with no manual copy; a Lidarr move/rename removing the OLD Box path) cannot be run, and the music retention ruling stays parked. ✅ **2026-10-19 lane T2 (oldsrv) — the key half of the owner action is DONE on the box; the registration half is still the owner's.** Keypair minted on oldsrv: `/root/.ssh/livebox_push` (ed25519, no passphrase, `0600`, dir `0700`) + `.pub` `0644`, fingerprint `SHA256:7aYt1jRhP8AdC6FnCNByVzbYIdJ2ib7dQRPzGcbOync`. **The public key to register on the live Box (Hetzner console, port 23):** `ssh-ed25519 AAAAC3NzaC1lZDI1NTE5AAAAIEJTJMOZIAMb3o7p7IAB3FEVwYHZlLuk6eKLIGf6LHnk storage-push-music@oldsrv.kogler.si` `/etc/storage/box_known_hosts` is seeded from `ssh-keyscan -p 23 u653411.your-storagebox.de`, root:root `0600`, three keys — ED25519 `SHA256:XqONwb1S0zuj5A1CDxpOSuD2hnAArV1A3wKY7Z3sdgM`, RSA `SHA256:EMlfI8GsRIfpVkoW1H2u0zYVpFGKkIMKHFZIRkf2ioI`, ECDSA `SHA256:oDHZqKXnoMtgvPBjjC57pcuFez28roaEuFcfwyg8O5c` — so the script's strict-host-key gate can pass on the first real run. **Nothing was pushed and nothing was armed:** `storage_push_music.enabled` is still `false`, `push-music.timer` is still `disabled`/`inactive`, and the deploy script's identity assert still precedes the mount assert, which is why the mount guard could not be exercised live before this key existed. **The Kopia music-master leg is untouched** — it still restarts a running container and stays parked for that reason. Remaining owner act: paste the line above into the Box, then set `storage_push_music.enabled: true`, converge `--tags storage --limit oldsrv.kogler.si`, `systemctl start push-music.service` once.|

**(B)** keep the upstream script for the Node bootstrap only (its own comment says it ensures a new-enough node) and never for the pi binary; **(C)** leave the leg and add a refusal — when the answering `pi` is not at the pin, STOP and print the owner action [docs/harness-pi-seats.md](docs/harness-pi-seats.md) §1 already prescribes. **The acceptance proof is the same three reads whichever is chosen:** (1) a from-zero run — on a clean box, or a sandboxed `HOME=` temp-dir arm in the script's own `--self-test`, which today has none for this leg — leaves `pi --version` == `pi_host_npm_version` and `bash scripts/harness/pi-self-update.sh --check --strict` rc 0, never a seat AHEAD of its pin; (2) the version the smoke test expects is READ from the pin through `pin()`, so grepping the tree for the string that printed `expected 1.3.0` finds nothing; (3) a second run of the same leg changes nothing and leaves NO second layout — `~/.pi/agent/install/` stays absent or empty on a pinned seat, because pinned-and-managed is the state `pi-self-update.sh` refuses to converge. ⛔ Not a fix: npm-installing a second layout underneath a managed seat — that is the exact state §1 names as an owner action. A seat that already has the empty `~/.pi/agent/install/{releases,staging}` dirs removes them on the seat (`rmdir`), not in a lane. · [docs/harness-pi-seats.md](docs/harness-pi-seats.md) §1 · [scripts/README.md](scripts/README.md) `install-pi-wsl` + `pi-self-update` rows · HD-1118 |

Its only shared-state surface is that temp dir, which leaves the same mechanism this item has now twice failed to localise — pool-level starvation of a fork-heavy arm, not a fixture defect. Four pool members flaking this way with two of them already fixed locally is pool-level starvation of fork-heavy arms on a busy box, which the row's own rule says is NOT a fixture defect: no speculative fix, no added timer, no new row. ✅ **What the row bought instead, because a red run that arrives without numbers is a mystery:** `validate-all.sh` now prints a `POOL DIAGNOSTIC (HD-1117)` block whenever ANY item fails — per failed item its own wall and how many sibling legs were in flight with it, plus the pool width, the core count and the host's load 1/5/15, all read from the start/END stamps the launcher already writes so the instrument costs one awk on a run that already failed. Silent on green; an unreadable `/proc/loadavg` prints SKIP, never a zero. Teeth measured both ways: a green `--only` run prints none of it, a planted failing item prints `POOL DIAGNOSTIC … pool 24 wide (GATE_NPAR 12) on 24 core(s); load 0.17 0.19 0.43` and names the item. The shorter tail `§Phase 99q` DOES redden — but as an ambiguity finding, because the retried `Phase` window matches eleven `Phase` headings in that document; so a wrong lettered ref is caught today by the target's heading census and not by any rule. (2) The RouterOS factory `public` community still answers on both MikroTiks alongside the vault item, because the converge templates add/set only the named community and never remove the factory one — `IaC/router/templates/crs328_converge.rsc.j2:118-122`, same shape at `rb4011_converge.rsc.j2:566-569` and `crs328_initial.rsc.j2:82-85`. · [observability.md](docs/observability.md) §SNMP coverage · [monitoring role](IaC/ansible/roles/monitoring/) |

| HD-1165 | 2 | AI + gate | 1 | **the `check_mode: false` rule must see the line as this repo writes it and then classify what it sees** — YAML ends a plain scalar at ` #`, so `check_mode: false   # read-back` IS false for Ansible, and an end-of-line-anchored detector is blind to every such line in the tree: a role that really writes during `--check` could keep the check-mode escape (condition 4 of [deployment-ansible.md](docs/deployment-ansible.md) §Self-converge guardrail) just by carrying a comment. Both halves landed in the gate: the pattern carries its comment, and the site is then classified by the carrying task — a read-only module (`stat`/`slurp`/`find`/`get_state`/`*_facts`) or a `command`/`shell` declaring `changed_when: false` is a read-back and leaves `check_safe: true` honest, anything else (including an unknown module and a `command` with no `changed_when`) is a WRITE that reddens naming `file:line`. What the read now is: `python3 scripts/gate/check_self_converge_guard.py` prints a `[check_mode: false]` inventory per lockout role, and cockpit reads **0 write / 8 read-back** where the anchored pattern saw zero; `roles/vps-hardening` reads 1 write (its `sshd_config` `lineinfile`, and that role already declares `check_safe: false`). The rule and the verdict table are in [deployment-ansible.md](docs/deployment-ansible.md) §Self-converge guardrail. ⏳ **This row cannot be deleted yet:** `prompt.md` still names it, and `check_todo_done.py --refs` fails a brief that points at a non-existent row — re-deriving brief ids is orchestrator work, not lane work (O2). ⛔ Setting cockpit to `check_safe: false` is not the fix and was not done: the declaration would be false, and it would refuse every legitimate `--check` of cockpit driven from the controller. · [docs/deployment-ansible.md](docs/deployment-ansible.md) · [`scripts/README.md`](scripts/README.md) |
